### 52. ★★★☆☆ No ‘Black Monday’? Nasdaq, S&P 500, Dow Futures Spike As Big Tech Earnings Loom This Week — Retail Stays Cautious - Yahoo Finance

- **分类**: 📈 市场
- **来源**: GN Markets
- **多源确认**: 1
- **时间**: 2026-10-19T04:07:00+00:00
- **发生了什么**: No ‘Black Monday’? Nasdaq, S&amp;P 500, Dow Futures Spike As Big Tech Earnings Loom This Week — Retail Stays Cautious&nbsp;&nbsp;Yahoo Finance

<details>
<summary><strong>原文 / 抓取到的正文</strong></summary>

No ‘Black Monday’? Nasdaq, S&P 500, Dow Futures Spike As Big Tech Earnings Loom This Week — Retail Stays Cautious
Nasdaq futures rise more than 1%, while S&P 500 futures gain 0.6%.
Netflix, SpaceX, Tesla and Meta report Q3 results this week.
Retail sentiment is mixed ahead of the 39th anniversary of the 1987 stock market crash.
U.S. stock index futures rose overnight heading into Monday after a grueling week for Wall Street, as investors looked to a heavyweight lineup of Big Tech earnings to restore momentum amid fears of an AI bubble.
As of 11:30 p.m. ET, S&P 500 futures were up 0.8% and Nasdaq futures rose more than 1%. Dow Jones Industrial Average futures gained 0.5%.
See what 10M+ investors are talking about. Get the Stocktwits Daily Rip for what retail is watching right now, free to your inbox
The bounce suggests some investors are buying the dip in tech ahead of third-quarter results from companies including Meta Platforms, Inc., Tesla, Inc. and Netflix, Inc.
The SPDR S&P 500 ETF Trust (SPY), Invesco QQQ Trust (QQQ) and SPDR Dow Jones Industrial Average ETF (DIA) Trust also rose around 1% in overnight trading.
The rebound followed a brutal week for major benchmarks, dented by a dip in neocloud operators and reports that frontier AI lab Anthropic's financial metrics were softer than expected. The tech-heavy Nasdaq Composite fell nearly 4.5% while the S&P 500 dropped 1.95%, the worst week for both since early June. The Dow bucked the trend, rising 0.67% for its second straight weekly gain.
Netflix reports after the bell on Tuesday and is expected to post earnings per share (EPS) of $0.82, up from $0.59 a year earlier, on revenue of $12.87 billion, according to Stocktwits data.
SpaceX also reports its second quarterly update as a public company Tuesday evening, with analysts expecting EPS of $0.14 on $12.8 billion in revenue.
Tesla and Meta report after the close on Wednesday. Tesla is expected to post EPS of $0.45 on $27.94 billion in revenue, down from $0.50 a share and $28.09 billion in revenue a year earlier.
Meta is expected to report EPS of $6.58 on revenue of $63.34 billion. Its year-earlier EPS of $1.05 was weighed down by a one-time tax charge of about $16 billion.
Other companies reporting this week include General Motors, Procter & Gamble, American Express, Lam Research and Texas Instruments.
Oil prices eased overnight. Brent crude futures fell nearly 1.9% to $107.88 a barrel, while West Texas Intermediate crude slipped 0.8% to $100.58. Prices had briefly spiked after Ukraine reportedly struck Russian oil infrastructure and reports of tanker disruptions in the Persian Gulf. The United States Oil Fund (USO) fell 1.6% overnight, with retail traders 'neutral' on the ETF.
Russian President Vladimir Putin has proposed a three-way summit with President Donald Trump and Chinese President Xi Jinping, The Wall Street Journal reported, citing people familiar with the matter.
Trump has not yet responded directly to the report but said in a Truth Social post late Sunday that he was holding off on a decision related to Russia to avoid jeopardizing peace negotiations between Moscow and Kyiv. He said progress was being made in the talks, including on hostage negotiations, and argued that signing the document in question at this stage could push Russia away from the negotiating table. Trump also said Moscow was providing limited assistance to Iran and that he wanted to keep it that way. "I WANT THIS WAR END!" he added.
In Asia, Japan's Nikkei fell 1.2%, while South Korea's Kospi rose 0.2% and Australia's S&P/ASX 200 gained 0.3%. China's Shanghai Composite rose 0.25% after official data showed third-quarter economic growth of 4.5% year-over-year, beating forecasts, driven by strong exports.
Retail sentiment on Stocktwits around QQQ was in 'neutral' territory, while sentiment around SPY and DIA was 'bearish,' with message volume at 'normal' levels.
Many users noted that Monday marks the 39th anniversary of the 1987 "Black Monday" crash, when the Dow and S&P 500 each lost more than 20% in a single session.
"QQQ highest volume on Friday since the early June tech-wreck. Would like to see a big green stick tomorrow," one user said .
Another user cautioned : "Guys relax let's not get excited... This can literally dump back before 9:30."
A Stocktwits poll of more than 12,500 respondents found 46% expect the S&P 500 to gain more than 1% in the week of Oct. 19-23, while 31% expect a drop of more than 1%.
Markets commentator The Kobeissi Letter said in a post that calls for a "Black Monday" redux were spreading rapidly on social media over the weekend, but added that it still expects the S&P 500 to hit 8,000.
"This is arguably the most resilient market in history. If the S&P 500 is 2% away from a record high despite inflation nearing 4%, gas prices on the rise, and the Fed HIKING rates, just imagine where the S&P 500 would be if the Iran War ended, oil prices came back down, and rate cuts returned. Or, if it never even happened," it said on X.
"Earnings growth just over the last 7 months of the Iran War has been historic, with the S&P 500 posting +52% blended earnings growth in Q2 2026."

### 53. ★★★☆☆ If a Stock Market Crash Is Coming, History Says This 1 Strategy Has a Perfect Track Record - The Motley Fool

- **分类**: 📈 市场
- **来源**: GN Markets
- **多源确认**: 1
- **时间**: 2026-10-19T09:08:53+00:00
- **发生了什么**: If a Stock Market Crash Is Coming, History Says This 1 Strategy Has a Perfect Track Record&nbsp;&nbsp;The Motley Fool

<details>
<summary><strong>原文 / 抓取到的正文</strong></summary>

No one knows for sure that a stock market crash is coming anytime soon. However, there are several unmistakable warning signs that turbulence could lie ahead. Treasury yields are rocking multi-decade highs. Stock valuations are at frothy levels by some metrics.
What should investors do if (and it&#x27;s only an if, at this point) a stock market crash really is coming? History&#x27;s answer is both simpler and more counterintuitive than you might expect.
Image source: Getty Images. Image source: Getty Images.
There&#x27;s one investing strategy that boasts a perfect track record. First, buy a low-cost S&P 500 ( ^GSPC -1.09% ) index fund such as the Vanguard S&P 500 ETF ( VOO -1.10% ) or the State Street SPDR Portfolio S&P 500 ETF ( SPYM -1.11% ) . Second, hold it for at least 20 years.
Following this approach in the past has always made money. The S&P 500 and its predecessors have never produced a negative 20-year total return. Not even during the Great Depression, World War II, the 1970s stagflation, the dot-com bubble&#x27;s burst, or the 2008 financial crisis. Every 20-year holding period ended with a positive gain.
By the way, holding an S&P 500 fund for 10 years has nearly as impressive a record. The S&P 500 has delivered a positive return 94% of the time over any 10-year stretch since 1928.
Why does this simple buy-and-hold strategy work so well? For one thing, the stock market has gone up more than it has gone down. Market crashes have always been followed (eventually) by strong rebounds.
Reinvested dividends are important, too. Roughly 44% of the S&P 500&#x27;s total return over the last 30 years stemmed from dividends.
The "low-cost" part of this strategy also matters. The Vanguard S&P 500 ETF&#x27;s annual expense ratio is 0.03%. The State Street SPDR Portfolio S&P 500 ETF&#x27;s expense ratio is even lower at 0.02%. With such low costs, you don&#x27;t have to worry about fees eating into your gains.
Every fund you buy will include a warning along the lines of, "Past performance is not indicative of future results." The U.S. Securities and Exchange Commission (SEC) requires fund managers to include the disclaimer.
This warning applies to the strategy of buying and holding an S&P 500 index fund. There&#x27;s no guarantee that an S&P 500 fund will generate a positive total return over the next 20 years.
That said, the underlying reasons for this strategy&#x27;s successful track record are likely to hold up going forward. The stock market will probably continue to rise over the long run (even if there are a few crashes along the way). Dividends will almost certainly continue to account for a significant portion of the S&P 500&#x27;s total return.
I suspect that time in the market will beat timing the market over the next two decades -- just as it has throughout history.

Prior to The Motley Fool, Keith was CEO of Constant Care Technology, a healthcare technology company; vice president of American HealthTech, a healthcare software company; and a director of operations for Blue Cross Blue Shield of Mississippi, a health insurer. He holds a B.S. in Industrial Engineering from Mississippi State University.

Share Ukraine launches over 1,000 drones at Russia, setting oil facilities ablaze on social media
Smoke rises above St Petersburg on October 18, 2026, following a Ukrainian air attack on the oil terminal in the Russian city [AFP]
At least one person has been killed, and five others injured after Ukraine launched more than 1,000 drones at Russia, one of Kyiv’s largest aerial strikes in the war.
St Petersburg Governor Alexander Beglov said firefighters were battling fires sparked by Sunday’s attacks on the St Petersburg oil terminal, the Ruchi oil depot and industrial warehouses in the Kolpinsky district south of the city.
list of 3 items list 1 of 3 Israel’s ties with Europe are fraying. Can its election change that?
list 3 of 3 Photos: Protests across France spiral into looting, blazes and clashes
The governor said the “massive” strike killed at least one person and injured five, and that several residential buildings and two houses were also hit.
The attack disrupted flights at St Petersburg’s main airport, one of the country’s busiest, which was restricting operations “for safety reasons”, Beglov said.
Russian e-commerce company Ozon said there was a fire at its logistics hub in St Petersburg. The internet company VK said drones attacked its data centre in the broader Leningrad region, adding that normal services were available to users.
Leningrad Governor Alexander Drozdenko said 145 drones had been shot down and about 20,000 people were without electricity following the drone raid.
The Ministry of Defence said 1,015 Ukrainian drones were neutralised overnight over 15 Russian regions, the annexed Ukrainian peninsula of Crimea and the Black Sea.
The strikes came as tensions between Kyiv and Washington deepened, with US President Donald Trump criticising Ukraine’s attacks on Russian oil infrastructure, and the prospect of further US sanctions against Moscow still uncertain.
On Sunday, Trump defended his Friday decision to put a hold on implementing new sanctions on Russia, saying he did not want to do anything that would “foolishly” drive Moscow “away from the bargaining table”.
In a social media post, the US president said he believed new sanctions would undermine negotiations to bring about a diplomatic end to Moscow’s war on Ukraine, refuting bipartisan criticism that he was favouring Russian President Vladimir Putin’s interests over Kyiv’s.
Trump also lashed out at Ukrainian President Volodymyr Zelenskyy, who has been critical of Trump’s recent outreach to Putin, for launching strikes on Russian energy infrastructure, claiming Zelenskyy “doesn’t want to end this war”.
Trump had announced on October 11 that Russia and Ukraine agreed to an “energy ceasefire” , but offered no details. Zelenskyy told The Financial Times that he was “done” with the ceasefire a few days later, after holding off on striking Russia’s energy facilities while Moscow had continued attacking Ukraine’s.
Zelenskyy wrote on X on Sunday that Russia had attacked Ukraine’s energy, oil and gas sectors 76 times in the week since Trump’s announcement.
Energy facilities in four Ukrainian regions were struck overnight, leaving tens of thousands of families without power, he said, accusing Russia of trying to “cause a blackout”.
“America should see that Russia is not taking any reciprocal steps in response to sanctions relief or agreements,” he said.
Speaking on NBC’s Meet the Press on Sunday, Zelenskyy said that waiving sanctions against Russia would strengthen Putin’s war effort. He also said US-Ukraine intelligence sharing had resumed after being “blocked for some period of time”.
Zelenskyy said that while he trusted Trump, siding with Moscow would be a tragedy and a “historic mistake” for Washington.
Later on Sunday, Zelenskyy said on social media that he had spoken with US presidential envoys Steve Witkoff and Jared Kushner about the results of an October 9 meeting between Washington and Kyiv’s negotiating teams in Miami, as well as the “next steps in diplomacy”.
Meanwhile, a Russian air strike ⁠killed ⁠a woman in the Ukrainian capital, Kyiv, overnight while also hitting a subway station and damaging a 25-storey residential ⁠building, Mayor Vitali Klitschko said on Telegram on Monday.
Rescuers work at an apartment building damaged during a Russian air strike in Zaporizhzhia, Ukraine, on October 17, 2026 [Reuters] The attack, which also hit non-residential buildings and a medical facility, ‌injured three other people in Kyiv, Klitschko said.
Two Russian attacks on the southern Ukrainian city of Zaporizhzhia killed a woman and wounded at least two other people, including a six-year-old girl, local officials said on Sunday.
In another post on X on Sunday, Zelenskyy said Russian strikes on residential buildings had killed two people and injured 12, including two children, in Volyn.
“This morning, there was a strike on an agricultural enterprise in Volyn. All necessary services are working at the site,” he said.
“In total, over this past week, the Russians have attacked Ukraine with almost 2,700 attack drones, 1,700 aerial bombs, and 66 missiles, most of them ballistic,” the Ukrainian president wrote.

Fuel prices are displayed at a gas station in Berlin, Germany, on October 1, 2026. Oil prices declined amid Middle East tensions, although benchmark crude prices remained above $99 per barrel, while Europe faced concerns over limited LNG supplies and the need to replenish natural gas reserves ahead of winter. Photo: Clemens Bilan/EPA/ANA-MPA
European natural gas prices approached €89 per megawatt-hour on Monday, October 19, amid concerns over winter supplies, while international oil prices declined.
According to Trading Economics, European gas was quoted at €88.92/MWh, an increase of 1.57%. The rise reflected concerns about limited liquefied natural gas (LNG) supplies from the Persian Gulf and difficulties replenishing European storage facilities ahead of winter.
The figures were based on over-the-counter and contract-for-difference instruments rather than direct exchange quotations.
Brent crude fell 1.5% to $108.27 per barrel in early Monday trading, while US West Texas Intermediate (WTI) declined 0.6% to $100.83, according to Moneycontrol, citing Bloomberg data.
Later market quotations from Trading Economics placed Brent at approximately $107.15 and WTI at $99.60.
Oil prices declined as traders assessed tensions in the Middle East alongside indications that regional supplies remained close to prewar levels.
Despite Monday's losses, both benchmarks remained above $99 per barrel.
European gas prices remained under pressure from restricted LNG availability and the need to replenish underground storage before winter.
Trading Economics identified potential competition between European and Asian buyers, colder weather and prolonged supply disruptions as factors that could contribute to further price increases .
Underground storage accounts for approximately 30% of the European Union's winter gas consumption, according to the Council of the EU.
In July 2025, the Council approved a two-year extension of gas storage requirements, maintaining a 90% filling target while allowing member states to meet it between October 1 and December 1.
The revised rules also introduced flexibility in response to difficult market conditions , while intermediate storage targets became indicative rather than mandatory.

### 60. ★★★☆☆ Better AI Chip Stock: Taiwan Semiconductor vs. Micron - The Motley Fool

- **分类**: 🤖 AI/科技
- **来源**: GN AI
- **多源确认**: 1
- **时间**: 2026-10-18T16:44:00+00:00
- **发生了什么**: Better AI Chip Stock: Taiwan Semiconductor vs. Micron&nbsp;&nbsp;The Motley Fool

<details>
<summary><strong>原文 / 抓取到的正文</strong></summary>

While companies like Nvidia and  AMD may get most of the headline glory in the AI realm for their powerful processors, the reality is these companies only design some of the chips that go into data centers. The fabrication muscle in the sector lies in a handful of chip foundry businesses, with Taiwan Semiconductor Manufacturing ( TSM -2.28% ) and Micron ( MU -5.63% ) being the two largest in the world by market cap.
But which one makes for the better buy? Let&#x27;s dig in, as these two are very different investments.
Micron and Taiwan Semiconductor aren&#x27;t competitors
On the surface, Micron and Taiwan Semiconductor are both chip foundry companies. Both take in client orders, then create their chips. However, that&#x27;s where the similarities end.
Micron and Taiwan Semiconductor produce two entirely different types of chips; Micron designs and makes memory chips, and Taiwan Semiconductor makes logic chips designed by other companies. Both are required in accelerated computing units like GPUs, but each serves a different role.
Furthermore, the technologies behind each one are radically different. There isn&#x27;t a ton that separates one memory chip from another. Take basic computer RAM, for example. Nobody cares a lot about which brand of RAM is in a laptop, only what capacity that RAM has. That&#x27;s because most memory chips are practically the same, although Micron is challenging that notion. It recently agreed to design an industry-first custom memory chip for Nvidia&#x27;s GPUs, which could be a different avenue to lock in better long-term contract deals.
Market cap Market cap calculated using publicly traded shares outstanding only. Does not include unlisted, private, or dual-class non-traded shares. Implied market cap may vary. $994.34B
Because one manufacturer&#x27;s memory chips are much like another&#x27;s, they are subject to commodity-like pricing. This year, demand for memory chips has steeply outpaced supply, causing prices to soar. The shortage is expected to get worse in 2027 and 2028, which would allow Micron to keep turning huge profits on its chips.
The logic chips that come out of Taiwan Semiconductor&#x27;s foundries are completely different. Companies like Nvidia and AMD design processors, and their various devices can deliver competitive edges on a range of criteria as the chipmakers work to improve speed, energy efficiency, transistor density, and more. Choosing a reliable chip manufacturer is a big part of this design process, and with TSMC&#x27;s massive production facilities, proven track record, and leading technology, it&#x27;s the partner that is most often selected. However, TSMC has relatively greater production capacity than the memory chip industry, and has been expanding production capacity in recent years, so it was able to mostly handle the AI demand rush.
Market cap Market cap calculated using publicly traded shares outstanding only. Does not include unlisted, private, or dual-class non-traded shares. Implied market cap may vary. $2.14T
Micron holds an edge in the revenue growth department, as it&#x27;s still benefiting from the effect of soaring memory chip prices due to the shortage. However, TSMC&#x27;s growth rate isn&#x27;t bad either.
TSM Revenue (Quarterly YoY Growth) data by YCharts.
Another area where Micron holds an edge is valuation. Investors today can pick up Micron shares at a much cheaper 5.3 times forward earnings compared to TSMC&#x27;s 24.
Why the huge price discrepancy? It all has to do with Micron operating in what has historically been a highly cyclical industry. Every time memory demand outpaces supply, the chipmakers start to build new fabs. By the time they come online, the demand waves have often slackened, supply leaps ahead, and prices tumble.
The market isn&#x27;t convinced that the current wave of intense memory chip demand driven by the AI infrastructure build-out will last, and it&#x27;s discounting it as a result. But Micron and others in the memory industry have said the shortage is going to get worse before it gets better. If Micron&#x27;s stock rises throughout the year due to strong demand, it could deliver incredible returns.
TSMC is also a solid investment, and because the logic chip industry is not as cyclical (it still is, but not to the same degree), the market is not as concerned about its outlook, and therefore is not valuing the stock at a discount.
I think this is a huge mismatch, and Micron makes for the better buy, at least when upside potential is considered. While TSMC is still a fantastic investment, the mismatch between Micron&#x27;s stock price and its earnings potential is just too much to ignore. Investors should pounce on the deal.

In addition to The Motley Fool, Keithen is a mechanical engineer and has held roles at Honeywell and smaller industrial companies like Brand Hydraulics and Lincoln Industries.
